## Appendix E: Changes in the English primary edition

This appendix lists every change made to this file when the English edition became the primary version, so that the Chinese reference translation can be brought up to date. Line numbers refer to the file before these changes (SHA-256 38a5cd1f11f7b70724f4706af67c38e77baec059433d7f9d5af700254d6f5655), which is line-aligned with the Chinese reference translation. Each item gives the line, the section, the text replaced (old → new) and the finding of the content review that caused it (report `content-1`–`content-4`, `content-global`; the full disposition of every finding is in `content-fixes-applied.md`). Changes to Appendix D (D7, D9, D14, D21 extended; D26–D80 added) and the addition of this appendix are listed at the end.

**Body changes**

1. Line 3 (Simulator parameter calibration workflow: abstract model, general architecture and specialization interface (v7.37.4 frozen version, clean edition)) [global N1]: “**This is the English translation** of the Traditional Chinese clean edition (`simulator-calibration-v7.37.4-clean.md`, SHA-256 92e0c8e5262536773c490f5a91f72e903f516d9fc05067aee81b42ceef459cf8), aligned with it line by line; where the two differ, the Chinese clean edition prevails.” → “**This English edition is the primary version.** The Traditional Chinese clean edition (`simulator-calibration-v7.37.4-clean.md`, SHA-256 92e0c8e5262536773c490f5a91f72e903f516d9fc05067aee81b42ceef459cf8) is kept as a reference translation without normative force. It was the line-aligned source of this file, and it corresponds to this English edition as it stood before the changes of the primary edition (SHA-256 38a5cd1f11f7b70724f4706af67c38e77baec059433d7f9d5af700254d6f5655); the changes listed in Appendix E have not yet been carried into it, and line alignment with it is kept where possible but is no longer guaranteed. Where the two differ, this English edition prevails and the difference is reported as a defect of the reference translation. Where this edition appears to differ from a rule of the frozen version, the difference is reported as a defect and resolved with the author (Appendix D); it is not decided by the implementer. In this file, "the original file" and "the original text" always mean the frozen version v7.37.4, never the Chinese clean edition.”

The English edition was translated from the Chinese clean edition line by line and checked in independent verification, re-check and third-pass review rounds; from this version on it is the primary version (line 3). Line 2364 (Effect of other κ's failure records on cross-validation) [global R3]: “- When there are only `pending` records, and their resolution would change the effective grade or the use (rule 2 does not hold, no other condition caps, and there is no cap from a central upstream or a grade-limiting flag), this result is `incomplete` (§10.2 rule 0) until they have all become `final` or `withdrawn`, and then it is re-judged. When the resolution does not change the outcome (for example V fails, the validation data are otherwise reused, or an upstream already caps), the common outcome is given directly. This makes the outcome independent of the time of the decision, and recomputation with the same κ is reproducible after the records are resolved. When the other κ never decides, the record stays at `pending`, and this result, in the cases where the resolution would change the outcome, stays `incomplete` until it decides or abandons.” → “- When there are only `pending` records: compute this result's outcome (effective grade, use) once with the records resolved as `final` and once as `withdrawn` (§10.2 rule 0). If the two differ (typically: rule 2 does not hold, no other condition caps, and no central upstream or grade-limiting flag caps), this result is `incomplete` until all such records are `final` or `withdrawn`, and is then re-judged; while the other κ neither decides nor abandons, the record stays `pending` and this result stays `incomplete`. If the two are the same (for example V fails, the validation data are otherwise reused, or an upstream already caps), that common outcome is given at once. So the outcome does not depend on when the other κ decides, and a recomputation with the same κ is reproducible once the records are resolved.”

72. Line 2890 (Completion of an ensemble) [content-4 F3, global R2, global F2, content-4 F17]: “  - Ensemble without replacement: n_req members. Cross-validation toys are renewed per §9.3: toys failing G5 do not count toward n_req. In member order, as soon as the number F of G5 failures totaled over all attempts exceeds 0.25 × (Σ_a n_req + X_max), that is the stop point, and V or P7 is `incomplete` per §9.3; Σ_a n_req is the sum of n_req over all attempts, and X_max is the ensemble's total k* minus 1 for an ensemble with replacement and 0 for an ensemble without replacement. An ensemble that does not reach this stop point is decided after completion by the total fraction per §9.3. This stop point is a choice of this section, to be confirmed by the author (§19).” → “  - Ensemble without replacement: n_req members. /   - **Cross-validation toy ensembles** (Ĉ_{r,f}, with replacement; the reference distribution of T_cv and the power toys, without replacement) are in addition renewed per §9.3: a toy failing G5 does not count toward n_req (nor, with replacement, toward the converged members of the stop point above). Let F_G5 be the number of G5 failures summed over all attempts, counted in member order. The stop point is also reached at the first member at which F_G5 > 0.25 × (Σ_a n_req + X_max), where Σ_a n_req is the sum of n_req over all attempts and X_max is the ensemble's summed k* minus 1 for an ensemble with replacement and 0 for one without; at that point V (or P7) is `incomplete` per §9.3. An ensemble that does not reach this stop point is decided after completion by the summed fraction per §9.3. This stop point is a choice of this section, to be confirmed by the author (§19).”

### Changes from the computation, g4sim-binding and triage pass

This part lists every change made by applying the computation reports `compute-D4.md`, `compute-D7.md`, `compute-D12.md`, `compute-D30.md`, `compute-D57.md`, `compute-D73.md`, `compute-19-2.md`, `compute-19-3.md`, `compute-19-13.md` and `compute-19-14-1f.md`, the g4sim binding check `g4sim-binding-check.md` (findings A1–A19, sections (B) and (C)) and the triage `triage.md` (all in `en/`; the disposition of every report item is in `apply-computations.md`). Line numbers refer to the file before this pass (SHA-256 6f413209010f0c80145ce9acf9ce3b78f6c6fb14b108a8bcfc2f4a0e88e32737). New rules introduced by a computation are tagged in the body "(settled by computation, en/compute-X.md; to be confirmed by the author)", so that the author can veto them. Item numbers continue from 112.

This decision of P5 is made at the design-stage θ_ref (Σ_boot is estimated only at that point). B ≥ max(500, 50p) makes this condition hold in the usual case (Σ_boot/Σ_data is about (p − d)/B ≤ 0.02).” → “- **P5**: Σ_boot / w is folded into σ_tot per §8.1, and P5 requires Σ_boot / w ⪯ f·Σ_data (w per §7.3, with ε̂⁺; the same f as for Σ_MC, §6.7), so that, as for Σ_MC, the bootstrap-noise term actually used in σ_tot is at most a fraction f/(1 + f) of the variance in any linear combination of parameters (en/compute-D7.md). This decision of P5 is made at the design-stage θ_ref (Σ_boot is estimated only at that point). B ≥ max(500, 50p) makes this condition hold in the usual case (Σ_boot/Σ_data is about (p − d)/B ≤ 0.02, so Σ_boot / w ≲ 0.03 Σ_data even at X = 4).”
118. Line 412 (Event-level parametric bootstrap) [compute-D7]: “P5 has already limited it to within f·Σ_data,” → “P5 has already limited Σ_boot / w to within f·Σ_data,”
119. Line 550 (Finite simulation statistics in non-Gaussian blocks) [compute-D30]: “- **Weighted MC**: Σw and Σw² for pass/fail and their cross covariance must be provided.” → “- **Weighted MC**: for each binomial bin, Σw, Σw² and Σw³ of the passing and of the failing events (W_P, S_P, T_P; W_F, S_F, T_F) and their cross covariance V_PF must be provided (V_PF = 0 when pass and fail are disjoint sets of events with independent weights). With W = W_P + W_F and ε̂ = W_P/W, the MC auxiliary binomial likelihood uses the variance-matched effective counts n_eff = ε̂(1 − ε̂) W² / [(1 − ε̂)² S_P − 2ε̂(1 − ε̂) V_PF + ε̂² S_F] and k_eff = n_eff ε̂, and, when ε̂ = 0 or 1, n_eff = W²/(S_P + S_F) and k_eff = n_eff ε̂. The likelihood is L(ε) ∝ ε^(k_eff) (1 − ε)^(n_eff − k_eff) with real k_eff and n_eff (its normalization cancels in every likelihood ratio). When Σw²/Σw is the same for pass and fail this reduces to n_eff = W²/(S_P + S_F), k_eff = n_eff·W_P/W; that reduced form must not be used when they differ, and separate effective counts W_P²/S_P and W_F²/S_F must not be used as the binomial counts (both fail the acceptance of §8.7) (settled by computation, en/compute-D30.md; to be confirmed by the author).”
120. Line 551 (Finite simulation statistics in non-Gaussian blocks) [compute-D30]: “- **Effective-count approximation**: must be validated by the simulation calibration of §8.7. G8 is affected by it, but within the range allowed by P5 its detection capability is limited; G8 passing does not mean that the approximation holds.” → “- **Effective-count approximation**: must be validated by the simulation calibration of §8.7. G8 is affected by it, but within the range allowed by P5 its detection capability is limited; G8 passing does not mean that the approximation holds. The acceptance of §8.7 covers a binomial bin when, computed from expected values, k_exp = n_eff·min(ε, 1 − ε) ≥ 30 and Δ_sk = |γ₁ − γ₁,bin| ≤ 0.1, where γ₁ = [(1 − ε)³ T_P − ε³ T_F] / [(1 − ε)² S_P + ε² S_F]^(3/2) and γ₁,bin = (1 − 2ε)/√(n_eff ε(1 − ε)). The expected values are computed before the fit from the per-class weight moments E[w], E[w²], E[w³] of a design-stage MC sample that does not overlap with the nominal MC (§6.6), scaled to the nominal expected counts of the bin; they are not computed from the nominal MC of that bin, because selecting bins on their own fluctuations biases the in-scope ensemble. A class whose weight distribution has no finite third moment (Pareto k ≥ 1/3 on the class weights) has Δ_sk = ∞. A bin outside this range falls under "Unvalidated approximation" below (settled by computation, en/compute-D30.md; to be confirmed by the author).”

125. Line 1013 (5.4 Model discrepancy V_model) [compute-19-3]: “- Principal components: u_k = √λ_k·e_k, taken until the cumulative eigenvalues reach ≥ 99% of tr R.” → “- Principal components: u_k = √λ_k·e_k, with (λ_k, e_k) the eigenpairs of V_model (the sum of its components) in decreasing order of λ_k. They are taken in this order up to the smallest K for which both conditions hold: /   1. the cumulative eigenvalues reach ≥ 99% of tr V_model; /   2. the discarded part V_⊥ = Σ_{k>K} λ_k e_k e_kᵀ, restricted to the calibration observables, satisfies tr(V_G⁻¹ V_⊥) ≤ 0.05. V_G is the covariance of the Gaussian blocks at θ_ref (§2.1), on N_d,c^⊥, without ν and η. For Poisson and binomial blocks, V_G⁻¹ is replaced by the expected Fisher information of the block in link space at θ_ref (settled by computation, en/compute-19-3.md; to be confirmed by the author). /   - Condition 2 is also evaluated on the validation observables, with the expected validation covariance (§9.2) in place of V_G (provisional, §19). All components may always be kept. /   - Why condition 2: condition 1 alone measures the discarded variance in link-space units. It ignores how precisely each observable group is measured, and with identity links it depends on the units. A discarded part that is ≤ 1% of tr V_model can still be many times the data error. It is then absorbed by no ν, and G6 rejects a calibration set that V_model describes correctly (on the toy detector, a G6 size of up to 0.75 at α = 0.01 with the 99% rule, and up to 0.07 with 99.9%). /   - Under condition 2 the size of G6 rises by at most 10% of α for every ndf ≥ 3 that P2 allows. /   - Truncation never lowers the power of G6: with fewer components the G6 statistic is at least as large for every data set, and the reference distribution does not change, because ν is constrained. Its only cost is size. /   - Basis: en/compute-19-3.md.”

For zero or near-zero responses the ratio is not used; even- and odd-order nonlinearity are checked with an absolute tolerance locked in the contract (in units of the total error of θ).” → “is ≤ c, default c = 0.1. c bounds the loss of precision, not the coverage: in the linear-Gaussian limit shift mode is unbiased and has nominal coverage for any information, and its total error exceeds the profile-mode error by a factor of at most √(1 + c) (§8.7 note 5). / 2. **Linearity**: for each component θ_i and each shift-mode φ_l, let D_il(k) = θ̂_i(φ_l = k) − θ̂_i(φ_l = 0), k = ±1, ±2 (the G9 refits; the other shift-mode φ at nominal), a_il = ½[D_il(+1) − D_il(−1)], and s_i = √(σ_tot,i² + Σ_l a_il²) the total error of θ_i including the shift terms. With c2,il = ½[D_il(+1) + D_il(−1)], c3,il = {½[D_il(+2) − D_il(−2)] − [D_il(+1) − D_il(−1)]}/6 and c1,il = a_il − c3,il (the cubic–quadratic interpolant through the four refits), the linearity measure is the excess of the prior-averaged second moment of the shift response over the linear model, Δ_i = [(Σ_l c2,il)² + Σ_l (2c2,il² + 4c1,il c3,il + 14c3,il²)] / s_i². The condition holds when Δ_i ≤ t_lin for every i, default t_lin = 0.06, locked in the contract. The test is one-sided: Δ_i < 0 (a response that flattens beyond ±1σ, so that the ±1σ responses overstate the spread) passes. No ratio and no separate rule for near-zero responses is used: a response that is small compared with s_i gives a small Δ_i (settled by computation, en/compute-19-2.md; to be confirmed by the author). It replaces the former tests "symmetry within 30%" and "Δθ̂(±2σ)/Δθ̂(±1σ) within 2 ± 0.3" (§8.7 note 5, D29).”

Lines 1639–1641 (7.4 Finding the extremum) [compute-D57]: “- At least 2d starting points, drawn with Sobol from the prior range. / - When the objective function is noisy, use SciPy's `differential_evolution` or the bundled NumPy CMA-ES, followed by local refinement. / - With multiple basins: each basin converges separately, all are reported, and the `multimodal` flag is added.” → “The rules of this section other than the second bullet are new (settled by computation, en/compute-D57.md; to be confirmed by the author); their tolerances are design values of §8.7 (note 6). /  / - At least max(2d, 16) starting points, drawn with scrambled Sobol from the prior range; the scramble seed comes from the seed block of the fit (§6.6), so each toy, pseudo-data set, fold and refit draws its own starts. The number is a design value (§8.7). / - When the objective function is noisy, use SciPy's `differential_evolution` or the bundled NumPy CMA-ES, followed by local refinement. / - **Converged endpoint**: a start whose minimization passes the EDM criterion of G1 with a positive-definite curvature. Starts that do not converge are recorded and form no basin. / - **Admission**: a converged endpoint is a basin only if σ_tot,i ≤ R_admit·σ_prior,i for every θ_i (R_admit = 1; σ_tot,i from the curvature at that endpoint, σ_prior,i per §8.3 and D54). An endpoint that fails it is a non-identified stationary point: it is recorded with its −2 ln L, supplies no θ̂ and is not a basin. If no endpoint is admitted, θ̂ is the lowest converged endpoint and G5 is decided there (it fails, since σ_post/σ_prior > 0.5). If a non-identified stationary point lies more than τ_tie below the lowest basin, G5 fails (contract). / - **Separation of basins**: the admitted endpoints are taken in increasing order of −2 ln L; each joins the first basin whose representative (its lowest endpoint) it is not separated from, and otherwise opens a new basin. Two endpoints are separated iff −2 ln L along the straight segment between them, evaluated at 19 equally spaced interior points with V fixed at its value at the representative, exceeds the larger of the two endpoint values by more than τ_bar = max(0.1, 4σ_noise). The difference of −2 ln L between the two endpoints is not a criterion: exactly degenerate basins have equal −2 ln L. / - **Reference-point iteration**: §7.3 is run from the representative of each basin. The −2 ln L of different basins are compared with the full Gaussian likelihood including ln det V_G at each basin's own convergence (the quantity of the ln det diagnostic of §7.3), because V differs between basins. A basin whose iteration ends at a point not separated from another basin is merged into that basin. / - **θ̂** is the representative of the basin with the lowest −2 ln L. Basins within τ_tie = max(0.01, 4σ_noise) of it are tied: θ̂ is then the tied basin first in lexicographic order of θ (contract parameter order), and the tie is recorded. θ̂ supplies every quantity defined at θ̂ (σ_tot, G2, G3, G5, G6, G9) and is the truth point of the toys of §7.3 "Toy data and V̂". / - **Multiple basins**: when two or more basins are admitted, G1 is decided for each basin, every basin is reported (θ, −2 ln L relative to θ̂, σ_tot), and the `multimodal` flag is added. / - **Toys, pseudo-data, folds, refits and scan points** use this section with the same number of starts and the same tolerances; each takes its own θ̂ by the rule above, and its q (§7.5) is referred to its own θ̂. A profile scan point is started, besides its own starts, from the representative of every basin of that fit with the scanned parameter set to the scan value.”

On the same Sobol scan points as the matrix condition, with that point's Asimov, compute /  /      ```latex /      b_J = -F^{-1}(t - u),\qquad /      t_l = \operatorname{tr}(W K_l),\qquad /      u_l = \sum_j \left( J_l^{T} W K_j q_j + J_j^{T} W K_l q_j \right), /      ``` /  /      with J the Jacobian of the prediction, W = (V_data + V_MC)⁻¹, F = JᵀWJ, and q_j the j-th column of WJF⁻¹. It is estimated from the per-event contributions in the same way as V_MC (for per-event weights in disjoint bins, K_l = ½ ∂V_MC/∂θ_l). The condition is |b_J,i| ≤ 0.15σ_tot,i^design for every parameter, taking the worst point. /      - The variance that the Jacobian noise adds at the fit point is already in Σ_MC, because V_MC is recomputed at the fit point (§7.3); no separate variance term is added. /      - When the noise of the nominal MC does not vary with θ, K_l = 0 and this item passes. /      - b_J scales as 1/N_MC: on failure, increase the MC by the factor |b_J,i|/(0.15σ_tot,i^design), or move the generation point toward the worst point (remedy level: plan). /      - Not defined for non-Gaussian blocks, or for MC predictions that are not smooth in θ (§19). /      - The 0.15 is a design value (§8.7), to be calibrated in phase 2: in the linear Gaussian configuration of §8.7 note 4 within the P5 range, the extra 68% undercoverage is ≤ 0.008 with it, while without it the loss reaches 0.031 (en/compute-19-13.md).”

After line 1979 (8.7 Thresholds to be calibrated in implementation) [compute-19-2, compute-D57]: new line(s) inserted: “5. **Shift mode** (calibrated on a reduced calorimeter-linearity toy with one material-thickness φ, label `synthetic`; en/compute-19-2.md): /    - c: shift-mode coverage is nominal for λ = 0.01–1 (deviation ≤ 0.006 at 68%, ≤ 0.003 at 95%, 10⁴ repetitions); the width ratio to profile mode equals √((σ_f² + b²)/(σ_f² + b²/(1+λ))) within 0.1%, and the rms central-value difference equals √(λ/(1+λ))|b| within 2%. The recomputation of condition 1 at θ̂ with c has false-alarm rate 0.50 at λ = c; with 1.5c it is 10⁻⁴. /    - t_lin: over 14 response forms (quadratic, cubic of both signs, exponential), r = |b|/σ_f = 0.5–4 and refit noise 0–0.03 s_i (4000 repetitions each), Δ_i ≤ 0.06 gives false-alarm rate 0.000 under linear and ≤ 0.011 under near-linear responses (Δ_true ≤ 0.03) at refit noise ≤ 0.01 s_i, rejects every configuration whose 95% coverage is below 0.936 and below profile mode, and the intervals that pass cover ≥ 0.668 / 0.935. Configurations with 0.06 < Δ_true ≲ 0.12 can be rejected although their coverage is acceptable (indifference band). The former rule (30%, 2 ± 0.3) rejected 14 of 42 acceptable configurations with probability 1 and passed a cubic response whose 95% coverage was 0.922. /    - Open: confirmation on the toy detector of §15.1 with the resimulation-level φ, more than one shift-mode φ (the sum over l in Δ_i), and a check of the refit reproducibility in a real fit. / 6. **Basins (§7.4)** (settled by computation, en/compute-D57.md; to be confirmed by the author): /    - On the bimodal variant of §15.1 (exact degeneracy, weakly broken degeneracy, close to the bifurcation of the two basins; ≥ 10³ toys each), confirm that the classification (the admitted basins matched by position, and the tied set of θ̂) is unchanged under 10 reseedings of the starts in ≥ 99% of toys; design-stage trial computation (en/compute-D57.md, d = 3, σ_noise = 0): 1.000 with 16 starts, 0.996–0.997 with 8, 0.907–0.970 with 2d = 6. /    - Report the distributions of σ_tot,i/σ_prior,i of admitted and non-admitted endpoints (trial computation: admitted ≤ 1.00, non-admitted ≥ 1.02) and confirm that R_admit separates them. /    - Repeat with an MC-noisy objective (σ_noise > 0, common random numbers and independent seeds) and confirm the stability with τ_bar and τ_tie as given. /    - Report the fraction of toys whose θ̂ lies outside the truth basin and the G8 bias; confirm that the toy-calibrated interval of §7.5 for `multimodal` results has G8 coverage within the targets of §8.4 (trial computation of the profile interval with the χ²₁ threshold: 0.929–0.936 at 95%). /    - Phase 3: confirm the classification on the degeneracy of the RICH replica (§15.1).”
156. Line 2146 (Data-side constraint check) [compute-D4]: “  - ρ_i^obs is the last-digit unit computed from the values actually read, according to the format declared by the release; for derived quantities it propagates along the lineage.” → “  - ρ_i^obs is computed from the values actually read, according to the format declared by the release. For a released value used directly, ρ_i^obs is its last-digit unit u_i. For a quantity y_i = f_i(x) that the workflow derives along the lineage from released values x_k, ρ_i^obs / 2 is the half-width, about the computed value f_i(x̃), of a guaranteed enclosure [lo_i, hi_i] of f_i over the rounding box Π_k [x̃_k − u_k/2, x̃_k + u_k/2]: ρ_i^obs / 2 = max(hi_i − f_i(x̃), f_i(x̃) − lo_i), rounded up. `calib_data.py` computes the enclosure step by step along the lineage, as the intersection of (i) the natural interval extension of f_i with outward rounding and (ii) the mean-value form f_i(x̃) + Σ_k G_ik·[−u_k/2, u_k/2], where G_ik is an interval enclosure, with outward rounding, of ∂f_i/∂x_k over the box. A tighter enclosure may be used only if it is equally guaranteed. A lineage step whose output is itself released and read starts again from the box of that read value. For linear f_i this gives exactly Σ_k |∂f_i/∂x_k| u_k / 2. First-order propagation Σ_k |∂f_i/∂x_k(x̃)| u_k / 2 is not used for nonlinear f_i: it is not an upper bound, and a correct release can exceed it (settled by computation, en/compute-D4.md; to be confirmed by the author). A lineage step that divides by, or takes the logarithm of, a box containing 0 has no rule yet (D4).”
157. Line 2148 (Data-side constraint check) [compute-D73]: “  - ρ_i of §2.1 is not used, because it is taken from the ±5σ_i⁽⁰⁾ window of the prediction and does not read the observed values; in a significant-digits format, when an observed value crosses a decimal boundary the actual rounding unit is larger, and a correct release with a correct model would also trigger (estimated at about 6%).” → “  - ρ_i of §2.1 is not used, because it is computed from the prediction and does not read the observed values. In a significant-digits format, a value read with a larger last-digit unit than the tolerance assumes would make a correct release with a correct model trigger. With the ±5σ_i⁽⁰⁾ window maximum of §2.1 this happens only for a value read beyond the window (at most about 9 × 10⁻⁷ per check for t = a + b in 3-significant-digit format); with the unit at the predicted central value it happens whenever the observed value crosses a decimal boundary that the prediction does not reach (about 6% per check for t = a + b in 3-significant-digit format when the prediction of t lies 1.3σ_t below a decimal boundary; at most about 2% for a true value uniform on the decade grid; en/compute-D73.md). ρ_i^obs makes the bound strict without depending on where the observed value falls.”

Line 2836 (Seed blocks and array tasks) [g4sim A3]: “For example, each (study, use, ensemble[, subset]) uses a `logical_run_seed` that is distinct within the plan (the "new seed" of §6.6 allows a different `logical_run_seed` in place of a non-overlapping interval), and member i of block b uses the global event interval [(b·m_blk + i)·N_ev, (b·m_blk + i + 1)·N_ev), where m_blk and N_ev are the plan-declared upper limit on members per block and upper limit on events per member. Block b of (study, use, ensemble[, subset]) uses `logical_run_seed` S_b = H(plan series identity, study, use, ensemble[, subset], b, t), where H is from `core/simulation/seeds.py` and t ≥ 0 is the smallest value that gives a seed not yet registered in the plan. Member i of the block uses the global event interval [i·N_ev, (i + 1)·N_ev), where m_blk and N_ev are the plan-declared upper limit on members per block and upper limit on events per member, and the plan must satisfy m_blk·N_ev ≤ 2³². The `run_seed` of each evaluation's `response-pipeline/1` equals its S_b (§6.6). H takes values in [1, 2³¹ − 1], so `calib_design.py` checks that the S_b of all registered blocks are pairwise distinct, and the registration record keeps t. Under the same `logical_run_seed`, f(use, ensemble) + b·Δ must not be used for different (use, ensemble): when b has no upper limit, these intervals eventually overlap.”

The G5 proportion of §9.3, summed over all attempts, is decided at the end of every attempt: at completion, and also when the attempt ends in a failing ratio test (§7.3); if it is > 20% there, V (or P7) is `incomplete` per §9.3 with remedy level plan, and no redo with a new seed block is made (settled by computation, en/compute-19-14-1f.md; to be confirmed by the author). Within an attempt, the stop point is also reached at the first member at which F_G5 > 0.25 × (Σ_a n_req + X_max), where Σ_a n_req is the sum of n_req over the attempts registered so far, this one included, and X_max is k*(N_tot) − 1 of the ratio test of this attempt for an ensemble with replacement and 0 for one without; from that member on, every continuation of the attempt ends with the proportion > 20% under either denominator of D68 (en/compute-19-14-1f.md), so V (or P7) is `incomplete` per §9.3 at that point. If the denominator proposed in D68 (toys for which G5 was decided) is adopted, the earlier stop F_G5 > 0.25 × Σ_a n_req is also valid; which threshold applies follows D68 (§19).”

224. Line 3910 (D4) [compute-D4]: appended at the end of the content cell: “**Partly settled by computation, see en/compute-D4.md.** ρ_i^obs of a derived quantity is propagated as the half-width of a guaranteed enclosure over the rounding box of its read inputs along the lineage (interval arithmetic with outward rounding, intersected with the mean-value form; §9.2, a new rule to be confirmed by the author); first-order propagation is not used, because it is not a bound (on the fixtures a correct release exceeded it at rates up to 2.5·10⁻³). The enclosure is exact for linear relations, gave 0 false triggers in 12 × 10⁶ correct releases (3 significant digits and fixed decimals, including decimal-boundary crossings), and caught every value shifted by at least twice the larger of the bounds of the correct and of the shifted release (the triage criterion "shifted by ≥ 2× the bound" is well posed only with that larger bound). Open for the author: a constraint whose enclosure is unbounded because a lineage step divides by, or takes the logarithm of, a box containing 0 (possible only in fixed-decimal formats, for a read value of 0 or a difference whose box straddles 0; it did not occur on the fixtures). Options: the constraint is uncheckable and passes vacuously, or a contract that declares a constraint over a derived quantity whose format admits such a box is rejected at locking.”
225. Line 3913 (D7) [compute-D7]: appended at the end of the content cell: “**Settled by computation, see en/compute-D7.md**: /w everywhere. Only Σ_boot / w ⪯ f·Σ_data bounds the bootstrap-noise term actually used in σ_tot to a share f/(1 + f) of the variance in every direction (the guarantee of §6.7); the raw form lets it reach (f/w)·Σ_data and, in a linear-Gaussian toy, broke the guarantee in 24–96% of its passes when P5 was near binding. At the default B ≥ max(500, 50p) the two forms give the same decisions. §2.1 ("P5" item and the reuse after the fit), §6.7 and §15.3.9 are now written with /w (Appendix E). See also D82.”
226. Line 3918 (D12) [compute-D12]: appended at the end of the content cell: “**Settled by computation, see en/compute-D12.md.** With V̂ frozen, the refit deviations have covariance exactly Σ_MC, so T ~ χ²_{d(K−1)} and the false-alarm rate is exactly 0.01 (simulated, 10⁴ repetitions: 0.0104 [0.0085, 0.0126] for d = 1, 0.0105 [0.0086, 0.0127] for d = 3); "0.000" is wrong and the reviewer's 0.01 is right. The regenerated value 0.033 depends on a setup the original file does not state, and it is not reproduced within its 95% interval (0.029 for d = 1, 0.042 for d = 3 at p = 50, B = 2500, K = 10, Σ_MC = 0.1 Σ_data). The sentence of §2.1 (ii) now states that setup, and that the extra spread grows with p only at fixed B. Open: only the wording, for the author to confirm.”

Line 3934 (D28) [g4sim (B)]: appended at the end of the content cell: “Plugin source (en/g4sim-binding-check.md (B)): not settled by g4sim, which has no mapping runs before S7, but constrained by it. The proposal can be implemented by writing the same `logical_run_seed` into every design point's mapping-run card, without `geant4_run.py scan` (which gives each point its own seed). With a shared seed, the Dirichlet replica spread of each point is conditional on correlated photon samples, so map errors are correlated between design points, and V_emu must not treat them as independent across points.”
230. Line 3935 (D29) [compute-19-2]: appended at the end of the content cell: “**Partly settled by computation, see en/compute-19-2.md**: the relative tests are replaced by one absolute, one-sided measure per component, Δ_i = [(Σ_l c2,il)² + Σ_l (2c2,il² + 4c1,il c3,il + 14c3,il²)] / s_i² ≤ t_lin = 0.06 (§5.5, a new rule to be confirmed by the author), computed from the four G9 refits with the symmetric response a_il; no near-zero switch is needed. The relative tests of the proposed reading rejected harmless or conservative nonlinearity with probability 1 and passed an expansive cubic response with 95% coverage 0.922; the 30% symmetry test was the only failing test in 0.05% of repetitions. An alternative with the same performance keeps two absolute measures (\|c2,i\|/s_i ≤ 0.15 and the expansive odd part 6 max(0, sign(a_i)·c3,i)/s_i ≤ 0.10), if the author prefers separate even and odd tests. Open: more than one shift-mode φ, confirmation on the toy detector, the refit reproducibility check (§8.7 note 5).”

232. Line 3936 (D30) [compute-D30]: appended at the end of the content cell: “**Settled by computation, see en/compute-D30.md** (new rules, to be confirmed by the author). n_eff and k_eff are the variance-matched effective counts n_eff = ε̂(1 − ε̂)W²/[(1 − ε̂)²S_P − 2ε̂(1 − ε̂)V_PF + ε̂²S_F], k_eff = n_eff ε̂ (Kish over pass ∪ fail only when ε̂ = 0 or 1); Kish over pass ∪ fail and separate Kish counts fail the §8.7 acceptance when Σw²/Σw differs between pass and fail. In toys a non-integer n_eff is handled by randomized rounding, n* = ⌊n_eff⌋ + Bernoulli(n_eff − ⌊n_eff⌋), k* ~ Binomial(n*, ε̃), fitted with the same likelihood. The acceptance (pull = signed root of the likelihood ratio, now stated in §8.7 note 4) holds for k_exp ≥ 30 and Δ_sk ≤ 0.1 computed from design values (175 of 175 configurations on fresh seeds); outside, the approximation is unvalidated (§2.1). Not part of D30 and still open with D33: the reuse of the toy auxiliary measurement across θ for a θ-dependent efficiency template.”

Line 3937 (D31) [plugin fact (triage), g4sim (B), A1, A3]: appended at the end of the content cell: “**Partly settled by plugin source** (`core/simulation/seeds.py`, `SimSeed.hh`, `core/simulation/rng.py`; en/g4sim-binding-check.md (B), A1, A3): event i of logical run S is seeded (H(S, i, 0), H(S, i, 1)), so for the Geant4 layer the proposal is right, and the checks of §6.6 now compare (`logical_run_seed`, global event interval) pairs. The response layer takes its Philox key from `run_seed` and `replica` of `response-pipeline/1`, not from S, so §6.6 now also requires `run_seed` = `logical_run_seed` in every evaluation and pairwise distinct `logical_run_seed` values (H takes values in [1, 2³¹ − 1]); the allocation example of §14.1 gives each block its own seed, because one seed carries at most 2³² events, and no longer presupposes this entry. Open: confirmation by the author of the response-layer requirement, which this workflow imposes because g4sim has no tie between the two seeds.”
234. Line 3939 (D33) [compute-D30]: appended at the end of the content cell: “See also D30: how the toy auxiliary measurement of a θ-dependent efficiency template is reused across θ (§6.6 "Reuse of the perturbation") belongs to this composition and is not settled by en/compute-D30.md.”

Line 3963 (D57) [compute-D57]: appended at the end of the content cell: “**Partly settled by computation, see en/compute-D57.md**: separation by the rise of −2 ln L along the segment between the endpoints (τ_bar = 0.1), admission only of endpoints with σ_tot,i ≤ σ_prior,i, θ̂ = the lowest basin compared with the full likelihood including ln det V_G, ties within τ_tie = 0.01 broken in lexicographic order, max(2d, 16) starts, every toy running the same multi-start and taking its own global minimum, and the union of intervals referred to the global minimum and toy-calibrated when `multimodal` (§7.4, §7.5, §8.7 row and note 6; new rules, to be confirmed by the author). The design value of the triage, "max_i \|Δθ_i\|/σ_tot,i > 0.5 and Δ(−2 ln L) > 0.1", is rejected: it merges exactly degenerate basins. Open: (1) the tolerances are design values of §8.7 note 6 and still need calibration in implementation (σ_noise > 0, where the terms max(·, 4σ_noise) were reasoned and not computed; V differing between basins; the coverage of the toy-calibrated interval; the RICH replica); (2) for the author, the remedy level of G8 under multimodality: the G8 bias test fails on every bimodal variant (the data pick the wrong basin in 9–48% of pseudo-data sets), which §8.2 assigns to the plan, while the cause is the contract (parameterization, a missing observable or the prior range); a note in §8.4 could give remedy level contract when the pseudo-data θ̂ fall in a non-truth basin; (3) endpoints on a physical boundary (G2) were not tested; (4) σ_prior of a uniform prior follows D54.”

The choice of denominator, and with it of the threshold, remains open.”

Line 3979 (D73) [compute-D73]: appended at the end of the content cell: “**Partly settled by computation, see en/compute-D73.md.** The 6% is the reviewer's estimate (RG2 F1 of the v7.37.3 regression; the original file attributes it to the reviewer) of the per-check false-trigger probability of a correct release under the old tolerance, for t = a + b in 3-significant-digit format. It is reproduced (6.1 ± 0.02%) only with the unit at the predicted central value and the prediction of t 1.3σ_t below a decimal boundary; with the ±5σ_i⁽⁰⁾ window maximum of §2.1 there are no triggers (≤ 9 × 10⁻⁷ per check), and with a uniform position on the decade grid at most about 2%. §9.2 now states the setup. Open for the author: the negative control of §15.3.5, which as written ("using the ρ_i of the prediction window as the tolerance") would show no triggers; proposed wording: "negative control: with the last-digit unit at the predicted central value as the tolerance and the prediction of t 1.3σ_t below the boundary, about 6% of checks trigger (with the §2.1 window maximum, none)".”
242. Line 3981 (D75) [plugin fact (triage), g4sim (B)]: appended at the end of the content cell: “**Settled by plugin source** (g4sim §9.1 and g4sim §8.8, `g4sim_simulate.py` `reweighted`; en/g4sim-binding-check.md (B)): g4sim's "reweighting" multiplies each deposit class by the visible-energy weight w(x) = 1/(1 + k_B x); it is a deterministic recomputation of `edep_visible` and forms no event weight. So the method is a deterministic rerun, G4 does not apply, and the discretization bound k_B(b − a)/(1 + k_B a) is the bound of §5.4. Table 1 of §11.4, §15.1 and §17 are written accordingly (Appendix E). g4sim does not export the bound (§5.4), so the evaluator calls `reweighting_bound` itself.”

This is consistent with "fails with increasing certainty" of §7.3, but the specification does not state it (side finding of en/compute-19-14-1f.md). Proposed resolution (proposed, to be confirmed by the author): state in §7.3 and §14.1 that such a redo is not registered and the ensemble is `incomplete` under that κ (remedy level: plan). [not triaged: added after the triage; blocks phase 2] |”

(p − d)/B understates the true ratio by 3% at B = 50p and by 10–20% at B ≈ 180–450; the statement "≤ 0.02 at B ≥ 50p" of §2.1 still holds in the toy (0.0185). ### Changes after the independent verification of the computation pass

This part lists every change made by applying the independent verification `en/apply-computations-verify.md` (findings M1–M4 and m1–m12, cited as [verify-c M1] etc.) and its note on §15.3.9 (section 7 of that report). Each finding was checked against its context and the cited sources (`core/partition/states.py`, `core/simulation/seeds.py`, `g4sim-task.md` §9.1, `compute-D4.md`, `compute-D57.md`, `compute-19-13.md`) before it was applied; where the wording differs from the report, the item says why. No class-1, 1?, 5 or 6 proposal, no D68 denominator and no D3 ordering was adopted. Line numbers are those of the file before these changes (commit c41f79e); the two lines inserted after line 1300 shift the later body lines by two.

247. Line 3229 (§15.3.9) [verify-c M1]: the G6 null-rate bullet restored to its text before the computation pass, with its negative control ("… With only B = 50p and no resampling, the exact size … and the decision is a rejection rate significantly > 1.5α.").
248. Line 3230 (§15.3.9) [verify-c M1]: the V_model-truncation bullet now ends at "(settled by computation, en/compute-19-3.md; to be confirmed by the author)."; the G6 negative control that had been appended to it is removed (it is back in line 3229). With items 247–248, the net change from the computation pass is the insertion of one line after old line 3179, which is what item 186 states, so item 186 is left unchanged.
249. Line 3166 (§15.3.5) [verify-c M2]: appended to the negative control "(the expected rate of this negative control is open: with the §2.1 window maximum the check does not trigger, §9.2; see D73)". The proposed wording stays in D73 for the author.
250. Line 2436 (§9.5 result table, ratio-test row) [verify-c M3]: after "redone as a summed redo with the next seed block according to §7.3" inserted ", unless, in cross-validation, the summed G5 proportion of that ensemble is > 20% at the end of the attempt (§9.3, rule settled by computation, en/compute-19-14-1f.md; to be confirmed by the author): then the following row applies and no redo is made". "In cross-validation" was added to the report's wording, because the row also covers holdout ensembles (T_v, Ĉ_r), which have no G5 renewal.
251. Line 1604 (§7.3, cross-validation Ĉ_{r,f} and T_cv reference toys) [verify-c M3]: the same exception added to "Within the same κ a summed redo … may be done as above"; "the following row" of the report is written "the §9.5 row for a G5 proportion > 20% applies (remedy level plan)", because §7.3 has no table.

D3 itself (the order of the two rows) is not decided.

Checked against the source; the report's text is used with the `not-submitted` clause added.
254. The triage tag is unchanged; the source row in `triage.md` (§19-14.1e) keeps its wrong order and is not edited.

### Author's acceptance of 2026-11-09

This part lists every change made by applying the author's instruction of 2026-11-09: "Accept all class-1 proposals as `triage.md` writes them, and the merges D13 → D63 and D64 → D22, except D68: I will decide its denominator together with §19-14.1f and D3." The accepted items are the 42 Appendix D entries of class 1 other than D68, the merged D13 and D64, and the §19 items §19-11 and §19-14.1c of `triage.md`. Where an entry of Appendix D and `triage.md` differ, `triage.md` was followed, and the difference is stated in the entry (D70, D76). Not applied, and still open: D68, D3, §19-14.1f, every item of class 1? (accept?), 3, 4, 5 or 6, D81, D82, and every rule tagged "(settled by computation, …; to be confirmed by the author)" or "(settled by plugin source, …; to be confirmed by the author)". No accepted item decides one of these; where an accepted item touches one, the entry says that it is not decided (D33 and D30; D61 and D68; D72 and D2, D3); D54 gives the σ_prior of a uniform prior to which open item (4) of D57 refers, and D57 is not edited. Line numbers refer to the file before this part (SHA-256 3795eec4eb8d46bf440071881e6f7686dfd75ef188227d897a559df650a316df). The item-by-item disposition is in `acceptance-applied.md`.

**Body, front matter, Appendix D and §19 changes**

279. Line 3 (line 3) [acceptance front matter]: “ **This English edition is the primary version.**” → “ The author's acceptance of 2026-11-09 (Appendix E, last part) made normative every class-1 proposal of `triage.md` except D68, together with the merges D13 → D63 and D64 → D22: these entries and §19 items begin "**Accepted by the author (2026-11-09).**", and their resolutions are written into the body; the tagged rules "(…; to be confirmed by the author)" are not affected by it. **This English edition is the primary version.**”.
280. ### g4sim S7 (hep-research 0.63.0)

This part lists every change made by applying the g4sim S7 check `en/g4sim-S7-check.md` (hep-research v0.63.0: g4sim S7 merged, partially complete; findings M1–M4 and m1–m9, the items of its §1, the obsolete statements O1–O11 of `g4sim-binding-check.md`, the rows C19–C22 and its notes for a g4sim design-error report). Each finding was checked against the cited g4sim files and lines before it was applied, and the scratch check behind M1 and m2 was rerun (it reproduces the reported offsets 0.14 and 1.46 map standard deviations, the schema error on `form.uniformity`, and the change of replica 1 when only `source_of_facts` changes). A requirement that this workflow imposes because of the S7 sources is tagged in the body "(settled by plugin source, en/g4sim-S7-check.md <finding>; to be confirmed by the author)"; corrections of g4sim facts are not tagged. Items accepted by the author on 2026-11-09 are touched only to correct or add g4sim facts: D28 (its plugin-source sentence, and its body text in §6.7: the event interval shared with the seed within a θ scan, and the Dirichlet replicas not shared), the §6.7 table cell that still contradicted the accepted D28 reading, and the open-entry list of line 38 (D83 added); no accepted resolution is changed. Line numbers refer to the file before this part (SHA-256 2b70a315ae5d54bc9d31ab6d5ffa5874c804804c2f73094d784890e151bb64a0). The item-by-item disposition is in `s7-applied.md`. Item numbers continue from 409.

**Body, front matter, §19 and Appendix D changes**

410. Line 3 (front matter) [S7 pass]: “that Appendix E lists under "Changes from the computation, g4sim-binding and triage pass";” → “that Appendix E lists under "Changes from the computation, g4sim-binding and triage pass" and "g4sim S7 (hep-research 0.63.0)";”. The S7 part named as a source of settled changes
411. Line 17 (front matter) [S7 pass]: “the accepted items are normative, and the items still open are listed in "How to read this document". |” → “the accepted items are normative, and the items still open are listed in "How to read this document". The g4sim S7 check against the hep-research v0.63.0 sources (`g4sim-S7-check.md`) was then applied (Appendix E, "g4sim S7 (hep-research 0.63.0)"; `s7-applied.md`). |”
412. Line 38 (How to read this document) [S7 D83]: “D73, D75, D81, D82, and every §19 item not marked accepted” → “D73, D75, D81–D83, and every §19 item not marked accepted”. The new open entry D83 added to the list of open entries
413. Line 865 (§5.1 Model identity, masked hash of `response-pipeline/1`) [S7 m9]: “Efficiency maps: in fixed-map mode, includes the efficiency map hash; in the mode where the map varies with θ, includes instead the settings of the mapping run (grid, the mapping-run specification other than photon number, and the masked hash of its g4sim specification), with the efficiency map hash masked (§6.7) |” → “Efficiency maps: in fixed-map mode, includes the efficiency map hash (`efficiency_map.map_sha256` of the map as built); in the mode where the map varies with θ, includes instead the settings of the mapping run (the segmentation, the masked hashes of the mapping run's three g4sim specifications, whose `run` block, holding the photon number as `run.events`, is excluded as above, and the component), with the efficiency map hash masked (§6.7). The map's own `hashes.mapping_run_id` is the mapping request's `request_id`, not its `run_id`, so the plan records the mapping run's `run_id`, seed and interval itself (settled by plugin source, en/g4sim-S7-check.md m9; to be confirmed by the author). |”
414. Line 1038 (§5.4 Known g4sim approximations, multi-component row) [S7 M4]: row rewritten per M4: g4sim S7 has neither the component split in the digitization nor a multi-component validation run; this workflow digitizes such a material itself (one stage name per component) and measures d_a in its own paired comparison; tagged; cites C19
415. Line 1042 (§5.4 Known g4sim approximations, efficiency-map row) [S7 m4]: “| Discrete model M (either enumeration or absorption); may also serve as an alternative hypothesis of §9.4 (of the "simulation setting" form) |” → “| Discrete model M (either enumeration or absorption); may also serve as an alternative hypothesis of §9.4 (of the "simulation setting" form). The full optical run of the comparison meets g4sim's validation conditions (`validation_findings`, `validation_response_findings`: Cerenkov off, RESOLUTIONSCALE = r, Birks declared, saturation on, by-particle-type off, nominal LY, no extra yield variation, one component) (settled by plugin source, en/g4sim-S7-check.md m4; to be confirmed by the author); Cherenkov light, where it matters, is a separate node-1 term (g4sim §10.4 option (a) or (b)) |”
416. Line 1253 (§6.6 Definitions when g4sim is used, Response sampling row) [S7 M2]: “so this workflow sets `run_seed` equal to the run card's `logical_run_seed` in every evaluation. |” → “so this workflow sets `run_seed` equal to the run card's `logical_run_seed` in every evaluation. Efficiency-map replicas (g4sim S7) have their own volume key, built from the first 60 bits of the map's SHA-256, the stage name `efficiency_map/<component>` and the replica number, with counter (event 0, bin). They do not depend on `run_seed` or on the event, so replica r of a map is the same in every run. |”
417. Line 1256 (§6.6 Definitions when g4sim is used, after New seed) [S7 M2]: after line 1256, new bullet "**`replica` is always 0**" (M2), tagged
418. Line 1268 (§6.6 Check, item 1) [S7 m3]: “the MC replicas of toys, ” → “the MC replicas of toys, the mapping runs of §6.7 (one per efficiency map; within a θ scan the design points share one (`logical_run_seed`, interval) by design, §6.7; settled by plugin source, en/g4sim-S7-check.md m3; to be confirmed by the author), ”
419. Line 1362 (§6.7 Three layers of MC error, map-varies-with-θ row) [S7 D28]: “| Yes (the mapping run is redone with a new seed at each point) |” → “| Yes (the mapping run is redone at each point; with new seeds for G7, G8 and validation) |”. Aligns the cell with the accepted D28 reading (shared seed within a θ scan)
420. Line 1366 (§6.7 efficiency map) [S7 m1]: “- g4sim produces ≥ 100 Dirichlet replicas for each efficiency map (g4sim §10.4).” → “- g4sim generates Dirichlet replicas of each efficiency map on demand: replica r ≥ 1 of a bin is `core/simulation/efficiency_map.efficiencies(map, bin, r)`, and `replicas(R)` refuses R < 100 (g4sim §10.4). No replica is stored; a replica is fixed by the map document and r.”
421. Line 1367 (§6.7 efficiency map, native coordinates) [S7 m1]: “(grid × channel × scintillation component, including "not detected": the grid efficiency vector of the mapped efficiency maps that g4sim S7 delivers; the S4 `efficiency-map/1` holds only analytic `uniform` and `linear` forms with no replicas)” → “(bin × channel × scintillation component: one mapped `efficiency-map/1` document per component, whose bins are those of the production segmentation and whose channels are its `channels` list, in that order; "not detected" is implied by 1 − Σ_c ε_c and is not a separate coordinate; channels that detected no photon in the mapping run are absent and have ε = 0)”
422. Line 1369 (§6.7 efficiency map, shared φ_map) [S7 M1]: “ε = ε̄ + B_map φ_map.” → “ε = ε_0 + B_map φ_map (ε_0 the nominal map, below).”. Consequence of M1, not listed in the report
423. Line 1370 (§6.7 efficiency map, nominal map) [S7 M1]: “  - Predictions always use ε̄ (the replica mean, that is, the Dirichlet posterior mean) as the nominal efficiency map, so the center and the basis are consistent.” → “  - Predictions use g4sim's nominal map (replica 0, ε_0 = k/n per bin and channel, the unbiased estimate) as the nominal efficiency map. The basis uses only the spread of the replicas (δ_r = ε_r − ε̄ above), so the center ε_0 and the covariance B_mapB_mapᵀ are consistent. g4sim's replicas are centered on the Jeffreys posterior mean (k + ½)/(n + (C+1)/2), which differs from k/n in the same direction in every bin (for a sum over B bins with K detected photons, by about B/(2√K) map standard deviations). Replicas are therefore never used as predictions without subtracting their mean, and ε̄ is never used as the nominal map. g4sim digitizes only at ε_0 or at a replica, so predictions at ε_0 + B_map φ_map are formed linearly from U (below), not by digitizing at that vector (§19, C20) (settled by plugin source, en/g4sim-S7-check.md M1; to be confirmed by the author).”
424. Line 1372 (§6.7 efficiency map, registration key) [S7 m2, M1]: “  - The registration key contains the efficiency map hash, the replica set hash, the coordinate order, the center ε̄ and the basis version.” → “  - The registration key contains the efficiency map hash (`efficiency_map.map_sha256` of the document exactly as `g4sim_efficiency_map.py build` wrote it, which also fixes every replica), the number of replicas R, the `CORE_VERSION` of the replica sampler, the coordinate order, the center ε_0 and the basis version. The map document is never rewritten, not even to drop its `form.uniformity` block (which `efficiency_map.json` does not list, so schema validation of a built map fails in g4sim 0.63.0, D83): any change of its bytes changes the hash and every replica (settled by plugin source, en/g4sim-S7-check.md m2; to be confirmed by the author).”
425. Line 1373 (§6.7 propagation in each study) [S7 M1]: “each study computes by re-digitization the response of the observables to each basis direction, U_k = ∂μ/∂ε · B_map[:, k] (link space; U is the observable × basis matrix)” → “each study computes by re-digitization the response of the observables to each basis direction, U_k = ∂μ/∂ε · B_map[:, k] (link space; U is the observable × basis matrix; with g4sim, from re-digitizations at the replicas with `replica` = 0 and `map_replica` = r: U = M V, M the matrix of the replica differences of μ divided by √(R−1), which is exact when μ is linear in ε, en/g4sim-S7-check.md §4)”. Consequence of M1 (how U is obtained when g4sim digitizes only at replicas), not listed in the report
426. Line 1384 (§6.7 Two efficiency map modes, first sub-item) [S7 D28]: “    - Each design point does its own mapping run, with a seed independent of the seeds of the other uses (G7, G8, validation; §6.6), and the efficiency map is a derived value of θ: its hash is masked from the model identity per §5.1, and the model identity uses the settings of the mapping run instead (except the photon number, see below).” → “    - Each design point does its own mapping run, and the efficiency map is a derived value of θ: its hash is masked from the model identity per §5.1, and the model identity uses the settings of the mapping run instead (except the photon number, see below). The proposal can be implemented by writing the same `logical_run_seed` into every design point's mapping-run card, without `geant4_run.py scan` (which gives each point its own seed). With a shared seed, the Dirichlet replica spread of each point is conditional on correlated photon samples, so map errors are correlated between design points, and V_emu must not treat them as independent across points.” → “Plugin source (en/g4sim-binding-check.md (B); en/g4sim-S7-check.md §1): not settled by g4sim, whose S7 mapping run takes its seed and event interval from its own run card (`g4sim_efficiency_map.py plan` writes only the request) and fixes no rule across design points. The proposal is implemented by writing the same `logical_run_seed` and the same event interval [0, N_map) into every design point's mapping-run card, without `geant4_run.py scan` (which gives each point its own seed). Photon i then starts with the same emission point, direction and energy at every design point, so the photon samples, and hence the map errors, are correlated between design points, and V_emu must not treat them as independent across points. The Dirichlet replicas are not shared: g4sim draws replica r from a key derived from the map's own SHA-256, so replica r of two design points are unrelated draws.”

### Verification of acceptance and S7; §19-g9

This part lists every change made by applying the independent verification of the acceptance pass and of the g4sim S7 pass (`en/accept-s7-verify.md`: major 1 and minors 1–17; minor 11 concerns `decision-sheet.md`, which was rebuilt from the markers of this file, and not the spec) and the computation of §19-g9 (`en/compute-19-g9.md`: the replacement text for §6.7 and §19, and its side finding on the number of replicas R, recorded as the new entry D84). Each finding was checked in context before it was applied. Nothing the author left open is decided here: D68, D3, §19-14.1f, the "accept?" items, the classes 3–6, the rules tagged "to be confirmed by the author", D81–D84 and §19-g4c stay open. The PDE thinning split of §19-g4c now reads as an option for the author in §11.4 Table 2 and in C20, as it already did in §11.4 (edge 3/4a) and §19. The new §6.7 condition is tagged "(settled by computation, en/compute-19-g9.md; to be confirmed by the author)"; the S7 requirements that the verification found untagged (the U = M V construction, the D28 event interval) are tagged "(settled by plugin source, …; to be confirmed by the author)". Accepted entries are touched only editorially (D14: one more symbol clash; D28: the tag of the event interval, which is not part of the acceptance; D57 (4): a cross-reference to the accepted D54). Line numbers refer to the file before this part (SHA-256 7091f3850c4d515bf6ec70a2b1c9acf746cd61bec58d5e9eaa802b9947899831). Item numbers continue from 454.

### D28 revision (author decision of 2026-11-09)

This part lists every change made by applying the study of D28 (`en/research-D28.md`: mapping-run seeds and replica coupling across the design points of a θ scan; synthetic fixture with g4sim's map and replica laws, Geant4 not run) after the author's decision of 2026-11-09, given in Chinese and rendered here (this file holds no CJK text): "proceed as recommended; put 'the true errors are highly correlated but the replicas are uncorrelated' in as a note". The replacement text of the report's §6 is applied as normative text: the shared seed stays the default and becomes conditional; workflow-drawn joint-pattern replicas keyed by the scan; one φ_map for the scan, profiled through the surrogate model; the analytic covariance for observables linear in ε; the fallback to the nearest design point's own g4sim replicas assuming full correlation; independent seeds only when the new P5 item passes for them; the new fifth P5 item. Its tags "(proposed revision of D28, research-D28.md; to be confirmed by the author)" and "(settled by computation, en/research-D28.md; to be confirmed by the author)" are therefore replaced by "D28, revised 2026-11-09" or by "Evidence, en/research-D28.md"; the P5 bounds s_i ≤ 0.25, κ_i ≤ 0.05 are tagged as design values of §8.7 (provisional thresholds, `provisional-thresholds`) and get a §8.7 row. The note the author asked for is a **Note** sub-item of §6.7 "Two efficiency map modes", referenced from D28. The open points of the report (en/research-D28.md §4.3, §7) are recorded as the new entry D85. Editorial choices: "emulator" in the report's text is written "surrogate model" (the term of this file); the bias range of independent seeds with the map term fixed at θ_ref is written 0.12–0.52σ in D28 as in the report's §0 and §6.7 text (the report's D28 text says 0.17–0.52σ); in §6.6 check item 1 the existing m3 tag (the mapping runs in the non-overlap check, a plugin-source rule) is kept, as the report's text keeps it; the event interval [0, N_map) of the shared seed, until now tagged "(settled by plugin source, en/g4sim-S7-check.md D28; to be confirmed by the author)", is stated untagged, because the report's replacement text, which the author adopted, states it so. Nothing else the author left open is decided here: D68, D3, §19-14.1f, the "accept?" items, the classes 3–6, the other rules tagged "to be confirmed by the author", D81–D85 and §19-g4c stay open; D83 (6) and the D84 cross-reference only add to open entries. Line numbers refer to the file before this part (SHA-256 d14b291ef7d5d2732c4c34f8ce4a0592a43cbaff96efb007169f6e446f73643a). Item numbers continue from 484.

### Verification of the D28 revision

This part lists every change made by applying the independent verification of the D28 revision (`en/d28-verify.md`: majors 1–4 and minors 1–16; minor 12 concerns `decision-sheet.md`, not the spec). Each finding was checked in context, against `en/research-D28.md`, `en/research-D28/tables.md` and its scripts, and against the g4sim sources of hep-research v0.65.0 (`CORE_VERSION` 1.13.0: `core/simulation/efficiency_map.py`, `rng.py`, `response.py`, `digitize.py`, `adapters/geant4/assets/g4sim_efficiency_map.py`). The changes implement the author's already approved D28 decision and are normative, without "to be confirmed" tags, except three choices that go beyond `research-D28.md`; these are tagged "(new choice of the D28 verification, en/d28-verify.md …; to be confirmed by the author)": the digitization route for predictions at a joint-pattern replica (major 1, as corrected below), the composition of C in the θ-noise item of P5 (major 4), and the repetition count of the §8.7 calibration (minor 9). Departures from the report's fixes, with the reason:
- **Major 1.** The report's premise, "g4sim has no entry point for a supplied efficiency vector", does not hold for v0.65.0: g4sim S8 added the digitization job `g4sim-digi-job/1` (`core/simulation/digitize.py`, run in C++ by `g4sim_digi`), whose `efficiencies` are given per bin as data (`check_job` only requires each value in [0, 1], one per channel, and a sum ≤ 1) and whose photon streams are keyed by the job's `run_seed` and `replica` exactly as in `response.scintillation_event` (the same `SubStream(key, event, volume, bin)`, `bin_photons` and multinomial). The report's linear construction U_j = J_j(ε_j^(r) − ε̄_j)/√(R − 1), with non-linear observables sent to the fallback and a new g4sim request in D83 (6)(b), is therefore not needed: predictions at a joint-pattern replica are digitized through that job, U_j = M_j/√(R − 1) holds as line 1404 defines it, and non-linear observables keep the joint-pattern replicas (the secant, as for g4sim's own replicas). The route is not in `research-D28.md` (the study evaluated predictions directly, `common.h_of_eps`), so it is tagged. The C20 row's g4sim state cell is corrected as a plugin fact. Not changed, for the author: the sentence of the M1 rule (line 1371, tagged "settled by plugin source, en/g4sim-S7-check.md M1; to be confirmed by the author") that "g4sim digitizes only at ε_0 or at a replica" describes the map-document route only; whether the fixed-map mode should keep the linear construction from U is left as tagged.
- **Major 2.** Applied with one clarification: two category codes are used, the per-map codes 1…C_j in the pattern tuples and the codes over the sorted union of the scan's channels for the identity 1 + c of g_rbc, so that one g_rbc serves every design point whose map has that category. The layout follows g4sim's own conventions (`rng._key` hashes `canonical_json(list(parts))` and takes its first 64 bits; `SubStream(key, event, identity)` puts the event in word 0 and the identity in words 1–2; `SubStream.gamma` refuses a shape below 1; `efficiency_map._gamma` uses Gamma(a + 1)·U^(1/a); `g4sim_efficiency_map.py build` sets `channels = sorted({…})` from the detecting channels), so it completes the key that the adopted text fixes and is not tagged.
- **Major 3.** Applied; "adjacent correlation ≥ 0.89" written as "0.886 or more", the value of `tables.md` at δ = 0.1.
- **Major 4.** Applied, with C = V_G + Σ_jl w_j w_l Σ_jl (V_G of §2.1, which already holds V_data^unc and V_MC) in place of the report's V_data^unc + V_MC + Σ_jl w_j w_l Σ_jl; the study's C was V_data + V_map (`emulator_noise.py`), so the inclusion of V_MC, V_emu and V_learn is tagged.
- **Minor 3.** The independent-seed factors are 0.736/0.324 = 2.3 and 0.176/0.034 = 5.2 in `tables.md` (P1d1 → Q1d1, seeding I); the report's 5.3 used the rounded 0.18. Written 5.2.
- **Minor 1.** Applied, and the D28 seeding rationale was made consistent with it: the efficiency gain holds between the oracle arms; with the required profiling, independent seeds gave a lower RMS in four of the six configurations (P2d1, P1d4, Q1d1, Q1d0) and a higher one in P1d1 and P1d0 (`tables.md`, I-G-prof against S-P-prof).
- **Minor 15.** Also applied to the copy of the note in D28.

The numbers flagged by the report were checked against `tables.md`: RMS 0.77–0.99 (I-orc: P2d1 0.77, P1d0 0.99), 0.88–1.20 (I-G-prof) against 1.01–1.15 (S-P-prof); G6 power gain 0.08–0.41 (I-orc − S-orc) and 0.04–0.34 (I-G-prof − S-P-prof); the P1d0 S-orc coverage 0.658/0.657 (68%) and 0.938/0.933 (95%), with S-P-prof the only P1d0 shared-seed arm passing G8; 2.8 and 8 (0.497 → 0.176, 0.138 → 0.017, seeding S, δ = 0.1); the I-G bias minimum 0.120 (Q1d0, ln LY), already written 0.12 in lines 1404 and 4080. Nothing the author left open is decided here: D68, D3, §19-14.1f, the "accept?" items, the classes 3–6, the rules tagged "to be confirmed by the author" (the M2 rule of line 1257 only has its scope narrowed to g4sim's own replicas, with its tag kept), and D81–D85 stay open (D83 and D85 are changed only as the report asks, D83 (6)(b) as corrected under major 1). Line numbers refer to the file before this part (SHA-256 80a7c78932d2c3db8d09dfc628c220c686f363ffb04cb3d9c81c77e35e4ac529). Item numbers continue from 516.

### g4sim S8 and S9 (hep-research 0.65.0)

This part lists every change made by applying the g4sim S8 and S9 check `en/g4sim-S8-S9-check.md` (hep-research v0.65.0: g4sim S0–S9 merged; S8 built `g4sim_digi` and the digitization job `g4sim-digi-job/1`, `placement_impl` and fast-simulation hooks; S9 added `condition-snapshot/1`, `alignment-overlay/1`, `g4sim_run.py diff` and `--overlay`). The report was written against the text before the verification of the D28 revision (Appendix E, previous part), so each finding was checked against the current text: line numbers were found again; the report's §6.7 W10 replacement was merged with the text the D28 verification had already put in line 1257; the report's C20 replacement was merged with the cell that item 536 had already corrected; the D28 entry, which the report finds not affected in substance, is not changed. Each statement of fact was checked against the cited g4sim file and line before it was applied (`core/simulation/digitize.py`, `conditions.py`; `core/geometry/alignment.py`; `contracts/schemas/condition_snapshot.json`, `alignment_overlay.json`; `adapters/geant4/assets/g4sim_digi/g4sim_digi.cc`, `geant4_run.py`, `g4sim_run.py`, `g4sim_simulate.py`, `g4sim_run/NewRun.cc`, `fast_sim_models.json`; the S8 and S9 gate records and `g4sim-performance-budget.md`); one fact was taken more precisely from the source than the report states it (a substitute fast-simulation model is validated under hep-ml's protocol by g4sim's own statement in `fast_sim_models.json`, so it is a fact, not a rule of this workflow).

Conventions. Corrections of g4sim facts are not tagged. Requirements that this workflow imposes because of the S8/S9 sources are tagged "(settled by plugin source, en/g4sim-S8-S9-check.md; to be confirmed by the author)": the job route for g4sim's own map replicas (line 1257), the checks of every digitization job (after line 1275), the `source_of_facts` of exported snapshots and overlays (lines 2717, 3719), the SHA-256 of `g4sim_digi` in the execution record (line 2919), the pilot measuring the response with the ensemble's implementation and conditions jobs in Python only (line 3007), and the entry of a misaligned geometry into phases 3–4 (line 3577). The report's proposals that would decide something for the author are written in place as proposals and recorded as new open entries: the folding of the alignment overlay into the detector-spec item and of the condition snapshot into the `response-pipeline/1` item of the §5.1 model identity, tagged "(proposed, to be confirmed by the author; D86)", and the binding of conditions and alignment to θ, φ, ψ, tagged "(proposed, to be confirmed by the author; D87)" and linked to D37. The M1 rule of line 1371 keeps its tag: its g4sim fact is corrected (the job route can digitize at any efficiency vector), and whether the fixed-map mode keeps the linear construction from U is recorded as the author's decision, not decided; the report's wording "while the linear construction stays the method" (W6) and "this workflow keeps the linear construction … and may digitize at given vectors for checks" (W5, C20) is therefore not applied. The PDE item of §19 (§19-g4c, an author decision) gets the second route, scaling the job's efficiencies by p, as an option only.

Findings applied: the 18 wrong statements W1–W18 (majors W1–W6, minors W7–W18): W1 → items 548, 550 and 590 (D86); W2 → items 559–560, 565 (D87), 591; W3 → item 582; W4 → items 547, 563; W5 → items 575–578 and 580–583; W6 → item 556; W7 → item 549; W8 → item 552; W9 → item 553; W10 → items 554–555; W11 → items 558, 566–567; W12 → item 568; W13 → item 564; W14 → items 569–570; W15 → item 571 (and items 561, 562, 582); W16 → item 572; W17 → items 559, 573; W18 → item 585. The report's §1 items not in W1–W18 (build_hash and the S8 features, item 551; D83 (1)–(6) still open in v0.65.0, items 557 and 587; D75, item 586; D84 and D85 cost, items 588–589; §19-g9, item 574; D83 (7)–(19), item 587). Statements of the earlier checks made obsolete (report §3, O1–O16) were corrected where this file relied on them: O1, O10, O11 (S8, S9 not started) in items 569, 570, 575; O2 (18 missing capabilities) in item 575; O3 (response implementation) in items 566–567; O4 (`placement_impl`) in item 549; O5, O15 (no driver) in item 576; O6 (re-digitization not cheaper than resimulation) in item 577; O7 (C12 state "—") in item 578; O8 (only `source_of_facts` required) in items 559, 573 and 579; O12 (ε only from the map document) in items 556 and 582; O13 (replicas only through `map_replica`) in item 554; O14 (one PDE route) in items 561, 562, 571 and 582; O9 and O16 are lists of the earlier check reports themselves, on which no text of this file relied.

Nothing the author left open is decided here: D68, D3, §19-14.1f, the "accept?" items (D37 among them, which D87 cites and which is not changed), the classes 3–6, the rules tagged "to be confirmed by the author" (the M1 rule of line 1371 and the M2 rule of line 1257 keep their tags; only their g4sim facts are corrected, and the M2 rule gets the job route, tagged), §19-g4c and D81–D85 stay open (D31, D75, D83, D84 and D85 get g4sim facts and the cost notes the report gives; D83 gets items (7)–(19) and its items (1)–(6) stay open). Nothing accepted on 2026-11-09 is changed: the §6.6 response-sampling row (line 1253), which holds accepted D28 text, only gets g4sim facts appended after it, and line 38 changes only in its list of open entries and of tags. Line numbers refer to the file before this part (SHA-256 33f8d92913e58d8348d478f37a639ad0a62bd2b0aee7ac4f7ce8ab099ed37c8b). Item numbers continue from 543.

### Verification of the D28 fixes and S8/S9

This part lists every change made by applying the independent verification `en/d28-s89-verify.md` of the two previous parts (the D28-verification fixes, items 517–543, and the g4sim S8 and S9 fixes, items 544–592): 1 major (M1) and 13 minors (m1–m13). Each finding was checked in context and against the g4sim sources of hep-research v0.65.0 (`CORE_VERSION` 1.13.0): `core/simulation/digitize.py` (`check_job` 37–74, `job_with_keys` 85–95, which raises `conditions-python-only` for a job with `conditions` at 88–89; `digitize` 107–170, which reads no `keys`, computes its own keys from `run_seed` and `replica` at 118–119 and 131, and counts the channel streams by `volume * count + at` at 152), `response.py` (`scintillation_event` 119–131 returns photoelectrons only and takes the yield factor from its caller; `yield_factor` 95–101), `efficiency_map.py` (`efficiencies` 141–151: replica 0 is k/n, replicas r ≥ 1 are drawn; `_gamma` 134–138) and `rng.py` (`_key` 59–61, `SubStream` 90). All findings hold.

Applied as the report gives them: m3, m4, m7 (merged into M1), m8, m9, m12, m13. Applied with a correction or an extension, and why:
- **M1.** The report's text, plus the reason that `job_with_keys` refuses a job with conditions; m7's wording of the event-time rule is used in it. The bullet stays tagged.
- **m1.** As given; r = 0 is stated as not drawn, and the scan category code is written u = 0, 1, 2, … so that the per-map pattern code c_j and u cannot be confused.
- **m2.** As given, with the reason charge and code are not compared (the job counts its channel streams by volume ID × C + c), and also applied in the `calib_design.py` row of §14, which states the same check. The (b″) tag of line 1405 is kept.
- **m5.** A new workflow requirement, so it is tagged "(settled by plugin source, en/g4sim-S8-S9-check.md; to be confirmed by the author)". It also states that `digitize.py` and `g4sim_digi` are one path: they use the same keys, counters and draw order (line 1253).
- **m6.** A new workflow requirement, tagged the same way. The report's text says "the hashed … snapshot", but only the D86 proposal hashes the snapshot, so the sentence says "hashed as proposed above, D86". The efficiency check also admits a joint-pattern replica, and p times the efficiencies only if the author adopts the PDE split of §19, so the check does not reject a route of §6.7 or decide the open §19-g4c. The job's `conditions` must be the snapshot. The job's SHA-256, which the report's text puts in the execution record, is added to the §14.1 execution-record cell, which did not list it. The check is also added to the `calib_design.py` row of §14 and to the new §15.3.19 test.
- **m10.** As given, with the m6 check added to the §14 bullet and to the first new §15.3.19 test.
- **m11.** As given; the stage cell of line 3574 also names S8, because its deliverable cell now does.
- **Beyond the report.** Two body statements relied on D86 and D87 as if they were adopted: line 2806 ("an overlay at every design point, the nominal one included: §5.1, D86") and C27 ("the plan records the times (§11.4, D87)"). Both are now made conditional on the proposal. D31 gets the g4sim fact behind M1.

New tagged rules: m5 (line 1255) and m6 (line 865). Nothing the author left open or accepted is changed apart from g4sim facts:
- D28 is accepted. Only the key serialization (m1) and the fallback source (m3) change in its text. Both were written by the D28 verification, and m3 restores the accepted wording "the nearest design point's replicas".
- D31, D86 and D87 stay open. §19-g4c and C20 stay open.
- The tags of lines 1257, 1276, 1375, 1405 and 2921 are kept.

### Author decision of 2026-11-09 (fixed-map mode)

This part lists every change made by applying the first part of the author's decision of 2026-11-09 on the M1 note of `decision-sheet.md` (given in Chinese; translation): "Proceed as recommended; the fixed-map mode need not keep the linear construction; the second PDE-split route should be researched and taken into consideration." Only the first part is applied here: the fixed-map mode need not keep the linear construction of U. The research on the second route of the PDE split (§19, PDE item; §19-g4c) is done separately, and no PDE text is changed.

How the decision is written:
- In the fixed-map mode U may be obtained by digitizing at each replica's per-bin efficiencies through `g4sim-digi-job/1`, with `replica` = 0 and otherwise the nominal job, under the rules that already apply to the jobs of joint-pattern replicas (the job checks of §6.6: keys for jobs without conditions, Python only for jobs with conditions; one digitization path within a readout system). U = M V then holds for every observable, as the secant over the replica spread when the observable is not linear in ε. The linear construction U = J B_map (columns J(ε^(r) − ε_0) centred over the replicas, that is J δ_r) is kept as an option only for observables linear in ε; the plan records the construction per observable, and `calib_design.py` rejects the linear construction for an observable not recorded as linear (remedy level: plan). Digitizing g4sim's own replicas through `map_replica` stays allowed. The k/n centre ε_0 is kept.
- The decided text begins "**Decided by the author (2026-11-09).**" and carries no "to be confirmed" tag. The tag "(settled by plugin source, en/g4sim-S7-check.md M1; to be confirmed by the author)" now closes the k/n-centre part of the M1 rule, which the decision does not cover; it stays a (b′) rule of `decision-sheet.md`. The other tags on the changed lines are kept (§6.6 lines 1255 and 1257, the S7 §4 tag of line 1375).
- Cost: building U by digitization takes R digitization jobs (R ≥ 100) over the study's events for each construction of U, where the linear construction takes none. They add no simulated events; §14.1 converts them into core hours with the pilot's response time (new bullet), and D84 notes it.
- Checked and left unchanged because they already agree: §5.1 (the job's efficiencies are the map's or the declared replica's, g4sim's own replicas included), §6.6 check of every digitization job, the joint-pattern replicas and the fallback of §6.7, D28, D85 (its point (3) concerns the mode where the map varies with θ), D83 (6)(b), the §19 PDE item and C6, and the local dimension reduction of §19.

Nothing else is decided: D68, D3, §19-14.1f, the "accept?" items, the classes 3–6, the other rules tagged "to be confirmed by the author" (the k/n centre of M1, M2, the S7 §4 rule of U = M V and the (b″) joint-replica choice among them), D86, D87 and the PDE split (§19-g4c) stay as they were.

626. After line 1372 (§6.7, new sub-item) [author decision]: “**Decided by the author (2026-11-09).** **U in the fixed-map mode.** The fixed-map mode need not keep the linear construction of U from the Jacobian (U = J B_map, below). g4sim's map-document route (`response.scintillation_event`) digitizes only at ε_0 or at one of g4sim's own replicas; its `g4sim-digi-job/1` route (g4sim S8, hep-research v0.65.0) takes per-bin efficiency vectors as data and can digitize at any vector with entries in [0, 1] and per-bin sums ≤ 1, in Python and in `g4sim_digi` (§19, C20). In the fixed-map mode, U (below) may therefore be obtained by digitizing at each replica's per-bin efficiencies through `g4sim-digi-job/1`: the job of replica r has the map's `channels` in that order and, per bin b, `efficiencies` ε^(r) = `efficiency_map.efficiencies(map, b, r)`, and otherwise the fields of the nominal job (`run_seed` = `logical_run_seed`, `replica` = 0, the same stages, transit and events), so that only the map changes. The same rules apply as to the jobs of the joint-pattern replicas of the mode where the map varies with θ (below): the checks of every digitization job of §6.6 (for a job without `conditions`, its `keys` equal those `digitize.job_with_keys` computes; a job with `conditions` carries no `keys` and is digitized by `core/simulation/digitize.py` only), and one digitization path within a readout system (§6.6 "Common random numbers"), the nominal digitization at ε_0 included. Column r of M is the difference of the predictions at replica r from their mean over the R replicas, and U = M V (below); for an observable not linear in ε this is the secant over the replica spread. Digitizing g4sim's own replicas through `map_replica` (§6.6) stays allowed. The linear construction, U = J B_map with J = ∂μ/∂ε at ε_0, that is, column r of M equal to J(ε^(r) − ε_0) centred over the R replicas (J δ_r, divided by √(R − 1)), is allowed as an option only for an observable that is linear in ε, for which it is exact and needs no digitization at the replicas; the plan records, per observable, which of the two constructions it uses, and `calib_design.py` rejects the linear construction for an observable that the plan does not record as linear in ε (remedy level: plan). In both cases the predictions keep the centre ε_0 = k/n (above), and the map term enters them as U φ_map, profiled with θ (below). Cost: each construction of U by digitization takes R digitization jobs over the study's events (R ≥ 100, D84), where the linear construction takes none; they add no simulated events (§6.7 event budget) and are converted into core hours by the pilot (§14.1 "Budget and scheduling limits").”
